The losers of black and blue pension logic are precisely those couples who have split up child care obligations, or those couples who, for example, have made a pension contribution split. One should actually think that such a thing might be seen positively and taken into account in such a pension adjustment, but unfortunately this is not the case. To illustrate this injustice, I show it by means of an example of calculation. With a traditional couple of pensioners, the wife, who has mainly taken care of child care at home, receives just 500 euros of his own and the husband perhaps 2 500 euros. In this example, both couples, who have opted for a pension payment split, may receive 1 500 euros of pension. Therefore, both couples would come to EUR 3 000 each. However, after the staged pension adjustment, the traditional couple receive an annual pension increase of 42 euros more than the couple who made a contribution split. Now let's assume that you would make another staged pension adjustment in 2020: Then that married couple who lived in such a way that the husband received more pension would receive just under 90 euros more of a pension than a married couple who made pension contributions. I just don't understand why, if you work out such a law and such an adjustment, you can't take this into account. [[Abg. ParlaMint-AT_2018-11-22-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00051_d7e12707	We're now on the 7th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Loacker is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). - Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-11-22-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00051_d7e12734	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! From the animals back to the humans. We have a much debated structural change in the Austrian health insurance landscape in front of us. The essential points for the insured, who do not notice anything about this reform, as Klubobmann Wöginger correctly said, remain the same, namely the differences in performance between the cash registers. Either you are in the public service or you are politicians, as we are, then you have a super insurance, maybe you have to pay once 5 or 7 euros for a doctor's visit, but you always get the better performance. You will now receive 17 Euro grant for the flu vaccination in winter, you will not get this as a CCC insurer. The differences remain. This is the reason why we have said that we let the insured decide, but let us let the citizens decide for themselves where they want to be insured, whether they want to be at the Austrian Health Insurance Office or at the official's insurance or as colleague Hammer at the hospital care facility for the national staff in Upper Austria. Let us choose people, let us give them the free choice! Mr Schwarz said in committee: Yes, but that results in some system for the party. – But that is not the case! Other countries also have such electoral systems. And what's going on there? – In the background, a risk-structure adjustment takes place so that the structural differences between the funds can be offset. One has more unemployed, the other has more older people, another has more good earners, and that is balanced in the background, so that all have the same starting point and it is only a matter of who manages better. These checkouts have a so-called counter-compulsory force in such countries. In other words, I must conclude this insurance contract with the citizen who wants to be insured with me, I cannot reject it, and so I ensure that all citizens can have a good health insurance and no one remains. In contrast to Austria, the decision-making party: In these countries, where there is such a free choice of cash, the insured are customers at once. Since they are not annoying, they are not connected four times as at the Wiener Landeskrankenkasse until they have a contact person, because they get an answer because they are customers. And if they are not satisfied, they can go somewhere else. There is no such thing as that in Austria. That is why we say: Let the insured finally select! Germany has this, Switzerland has it, the Netherlands have it, many countries with modern systems have such a free choice that an additional advantage with the following is due: People don't just decide on the money – where it's cheaper, I'm going to go – no, people are looking at exactly how the quality of care they get from this one and from that cash box, which is the better offer. This will give you an incentive for more innovation in the health system. Because not everything comes automatically and people are not compulsorily insured, the providers are invited – and will do so in such an insurance market – to make a better offer, and then the insurers really benefit there. In contrast to this social security structural change, the insured persons actually have some of it. Therefore, NEOS' call: Give people the freedom to decide, give people the freedom to choose at which checkout they want their health insurance!

ParlaMint-AT_2018-11-22-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00051_d7e12807	Madam President. Dear Ladies and gentlemen in plenary, gallery, internet and social media! The debate on insurance obligations versus compulsory insurance is a long-term and never really wants to end the insurance obligation. Our health and social system describes solidarity as the top principle. This means that citizens are not only responsible for themselves, but they are seen as members of a community and are supporting each other and giving each other assistance. And this solidarity is the most important and central principle of social security. In our system of compulsory insurance, the insured person is assigned to an insurance company according to his profession, but any further insurance is voluntary. What does this compulsory insurance guarantee? – It guarantees the same benefits for all entitled persons, regardless of age, gender and predisease. So there is no risk exclusion. It guarantees social justice through wage, salary and pension-related health insurance contributions, and guarantees a legal entitlement to benefits. The benefits of health insurance schemes are regulated by law, and there is also, to a large extent, a enforceable legal claim. As part of the current social security reform – this is not a secret – we are now trying to narrow down the entire Austrian social security structure, make it more efficient. What a huge loss of power this means for some of the Members present here, we have already heard through the many, many, even very unqualified, reports in one of the last debates on the part of the Socialists. I find it a little difficult here to use the word social democrats. [[Congratulations with the FPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! What do we want to achieve with this social security reform? We want to modernise achievements, we want to harmonise them and we want to have the same services for the same contributions throughout Austria. A departure from compulsory insurance to an insurance obligation would be not only the idea of solidarity, but also social security in the event of illness. In this case, it should be noted that, in the This is not the aim of our health policy. We stand for solidarity and inclusive health care for every citizen.

We currently have a 15-hectare seal a day. The Federal Government in the early zeros – I think it was 2004 – has set itself a target of 2.5 hectares a day. You see how far away we are 15 years later. Why is that so important? – It is important because every floor we seal needs 100 to 200 years per cent of the humus structure if we want to use it again. This means that if we grow something today, the land is no longer usable for many hundred years of agriculture. It is important because the threat to biodiversity is actually associated with sealing. We have just heard about a new study yesterday, after which 70 percent of vertebrates have disappeared in Austria over the past 30 years. Insect deaths are another keyword. In fact, diversity is linked to the vitality of our farms. We need to think more about, besides production, there must be a protection, a form of observation, a form of early warning system in terms of biodiversity, at farms. The third essential point, which is also central in the field of land sealing from today's point of view, is the question: What happens to a country that is much stronger than Germany or Switzerland, for example? – We have a higher heat wave, we have a higher flood risk, we have many disadvantages for the population and we have many possibilities when we quickly stop the surface sealing in the future. I call for this to be done. appropriate support. Thank you.

Since accession, the European Union has made a positive trade balance for Austria, but since Austria's accession to the EU, more than 25 percent of our farms have ceased to operate. In figures, these are more than 70 000 businesses. The causes are numerous: One of these is the deterioration of milk and meat prices. Our products are nothing more valuable, we live only by EU subsidies, we hear our suffering-tested farmers and farmers complaining. However, in recent years, as the Green Report shows, milk and meat prices have recovered. After many years of thirst, our farmers, who truly deserve our utmost respect, can finally rejoice again at the increase in earnings, and we are happy with them. [[Call the ÖVP:Thank you!]] But while they have a cause for rejoicing, other harvest yields are losing. We have heard of the catastrophic potato harvest this year, and up to 25 percent of the beet harvest has been destroyed by the beet-reefer. In the case of the organic beet, it is up to 90 percent harvest loss. There is also a cause for climate change, because of the much too warm winter, too few pests are frozen, and in spring they multiply like wild. Conclusion: The area affected by crop damage increased in Austria from 700 to incredible 12,000 hectares in a single year. In order to compensate for this damage, many farmers flexibly resort to the chemical culture. We must bear in mind that there are already areas in North America that are larger than Austria and which have grown with chemical-resistant weeds. There's nothing more to help with that. It can't be that the handle to the chemical keule is our only option. Another aspect is monoculture. We know that the formation of plagues is promoted Biblically by monocultures. We see this for example with fift monocultures, they are a true Eldorado for the book printer pest. The entire forests are thus reddished, including the protected forests that protect our roads from slides and walls. The economic damage is not limited only to the farmers. Who else remembers Galtür? Ladies and gentlemen, we finally need an antidote, and that is where the technology can help. The technology must and will remedy: Hightech also in agriculture. Organic agriculture is important, but it is simply not enough in the fight against the pests. According to the Green Report, the volume of research and technical innovation in this area has increased in recent years. Just as the government properly invests in the expansion of the broadband network and in digitization and lifts demonstration research projects in agriculture such as the digital farm from baptism, we finally need to invest in tangible alternatives to chemistry. The use of pesticides should then be operated only more in acute situations as an emergency solution and no longer as a permanent solution, because this simply destroys our soils. Large farms like Monsanto – today Bayer – have left important insects with poisons, such as our bees. An agricultural research billion could help to find a meaningful alternative to the chemical keule and to transform it into sustainable, ecological and climate-friendly agriculture. I hope that this idea is of interest to you, and I will hold individual talks in this regard. I hope that this will also be the subject of committee discussion. – Thank you very much for the attention.

Point Report of the Committee on Research, Innovation and Digitalisation on the Government Template (294 d.a._Other Organiser Bundesgesetz, which amends the Markenschutzgesetz 1970 (361 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2018-11-22-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00051_d7e20175	This leads us to the 15th place. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Dipl.-Ing. Gerhard Deimek.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-11-22-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00051_d7e20205	Madam President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The present draft is the second and important step of some substantial changes in the protection of trademarks. It is true that we have to do with new brand forms – we could already see this in the past at the first of these steps. Stamps no longer need to be displayed graphically. That is, everything we have in new digital processes – namely all these multimedia brands and multimedia technologies – can now be taken into account in brand law. Another important point of this innovation is the extension of the legal protection system. There is a really accurate examination before the brand is registered. There is an extension to the grounds for opposition and, of course, to the grounds of cancellation. That is to say, we have a much safer starting point for the registration of trademarks or for the granting of trademark rights than has been the case so far. Another and not unimportant point is – and this is the consequence of all brand rights – the contribution to the fight against piracy: Goods that are right-wing; we know the copies of important, modern, beautiful, well-designed products. Of course, it does not only concern such products, but also products in a multimedia sense. They can already be stopped by the customs authorities during their transit procedure, in order not to place them on the market with us or in a third country. That's an additional point. Last, but not least, also an important point: the better information about the data relating to license and deposit rights. To sum up, it is a very good, very balanced and very important step after the first step we have taken to modernize this new brand law. – Mr Federal Minister, thank you very much for this!

ParlaMint-AT_2018-11-22-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00051_d7e20794	Madam President. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen! Today we are dealing with a request from the government groups – Mr Zinggl has already mentioned this – which has also found the approval of SPÖs in the Committee. A warm thank you for that! Thank you also to the NEOS and to JETZT for allowing us to make a joint proposal in the Committee on Social Affairs and Employment. Short-term rental for tourist purposes. We all seem to agree that it should not be for non-profit housing to be used touristically. Social housing is particularly important to provide people with low incomes of high-quality but low-cost housing. We will therefore not allow social housing to be estranged from individuals who have discovered a lucrative business model in it. Back to the main request. I would like to predict that this request is purely about matters of non-profit housing. A few general remarks on this: The housing public utility is an essential pillar of the Austrian housing economy. It has a significant share in the fact that in such a wealthy country as Austria, you can live relatively cheaply, as is demonstrated by international studies. However, housing public utility is also faced with a number of challenges. There have been and are repeated attempts at profit-orientated companies to do business with non-profit assets, and that is what it is important to stop. As early as the spring of this year, we have passed a law to effectively prevent so-called circumvention operations. However, it has also shown that the existing instruments of supervisory authorities intended to prevent such things are not sufficient. We must therefore strengthen supervision – that is also one of the objectives that we want to achieve with this proposal. Another key challenge is the cost of rehabilitating the stock, in particular with regard to necessary measures in the sense of climate protection, as well as investment in the modernisation of buildings. The essence of non-profit housing is the cost-effective principle. In short: All expenses incurred by non-profit builders shall be reimbursed by residents in the form of monthly rents. We want to ensure that these conservation and improvement measures can be implemented without additional burden on residents. Furthermore, the application also refers to the housing investment bank, called WBIB. The meaning of the WBIB is, I believe, undisputed. Common construction projects are financed in the long term, and this is precisely the aim of the WBIB, namely to serve the builders with long-term, interest-based loans. We started a kind of transition of the WBIB in spring. Now we want to figure out whether the WBIB's ownership can also be extended to re-establish the bank accordingly. Finally, I would like to address a point that is particularly important to me or the EIA, namely the question of renting versus property. Really, it's supposed to be called: Rental and property. Austria needs both: Rental and property. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the FPÖ.]] Because there has been some significant price increases in recent years, countermeasures are needed to enable rent and property. This also applies to the field of public utility. We support both of them, both for a permanent socially bound housing building as well as for the possibility of building property in the form of strengthening the rental model. Thank you very much.
